In 1950 , January 17 was the day an 11-member robbery gang committed what was then deemed the " crime of the century " after breaking into a Brink 's security building in Boston and made away with US$2.7 million ( now valued at around $29.5m or AUD$42.7m ) . The historic theft left investigators confused , in part due to the low number of clues left behind in the security building 's vault . <p> http : **40;15509;TOOLONG ... It was later revealed that the robbers had managed to duplicate locks held within the building 's heavy doors and then , dressed in Brink 's uniforms , seven of the 11 entered the building posing as staff members.Within a 35-minute window , they had gagged multiple employees and forced them to handover money that had been counted . <p> http : **40;15551;TOOLONG ... Joseph " Big Joe " McGinnis was the originator of the heist , according to evidence later obtained by investigators.Despite that , a race against the US statute of limitations saw police rush to arrest and arraign each of the gang members as having taken part in the crime.Ultimately , eight of the gang 's members received maximum sentences of life imprisonment and just $58,000 of the $2.7 million was recovered . <p> http : **40;15593;TOOLONG ... In 1929 , January 17 was the day that the now-world-famous comic strip ' Popeye the Sailor ' was introduced to readers via King Features ' daily publication , ' Thimble Theatre ' . The character was initially included in the strip as a minor inclusion to the Thimble Theatre world , however he rapidly became the focus - making the franchise one of King Features ' most profitable and popular selling points of the 1930s.In the eight decades since his crearion , Popeye has appeared in comic books , television cartoons , arcade and video games , hundreds of advertisements , featured in large numbers of products including spinach and candy cigarettes and also ' starred ' in a 1980 live-action film that included Robin Williams . <p> http : **40;15635;TOOLONG ... January 17 is also the day in 1942 that Cassius Marcellus Clay Jr - or the ' Greatest of All Time ' Muhammad Ali - was born in Louisville , Kentucky , USA.Ali kick-started his boxing career by the age of 12 and had won an Olympic Gold Medal by 18 . By 22 , he was a world heavyweight champion as aprofessional in the sport and his career was sky rocketing.He is globally accepted as the leading heavyweight boxer of the 20th century and his joint records of beating 21 boxers for the world heavyweight title and winning 14 unified title bouts stood for 35 years straight . <p> http : **40;15677;TOOLONG ... In 1991 , the United States of America officially launched widespread military combat against Iraq with Operation Desert Storm , commonly known as the Gulf War.The war was waged by coalition forces comprised from 35 different nations , led by the US , and was sparked by Iraq 's invasion of Kuwait after lengthy oil pricing and production disputes . <p> http : **40;15719;TOOLONG ... From January 16 , 1991 onwards the USA 's combined forces subjected Iraq to 42 consecutive days and nights of bombings - one of the most intensive air bombardments in military history . The coalition flew over 100,000 sorties , dropped around 88,500 tons of bombs and widely destroyed Iraqi military and civilian infrastructure . <p> http : **40;15761;TOOLONG ... After the lengthy amount of bombings , a ground assault began that ultimately led to a decisive victory for the USA under then President George H. W. Bush.The coalition ceased its advance on Iraqi territories and declared a ceasefire 100 hours after the ground campaign started.Despite that , the USA and Kuwait suffered around 5000 fatalities during the combat and up to 50,000 Iraqis were killed in the war . <p> http : **40;15803;TOOLONG ... January 16 was also the day in 2003 that NASA 's historic Space Shuttle Columbia was launched on its tragic final-ever mission designed to conduct a series of scientific and atmospheric experiments.In its lifetime , Columbia served NASA for over 22 years and completed 27 missions before its final task was marred by disaster . <p> http : **40;15845;TOOLONG ... After 16 days in orbit , Columbia was destroyed upon re-entry into Earth 's atmosphere due to a hole that had been punctured in the spacecraft 's wing.Gases that were able to enter the shuttle through that hole then led to the complete shutdown of its hydraulic and control systems and sparking the break-up of the vehicle entirely.Columbia disintegrated into more than 84,000 separate pieces of debris and all seven crew members who were on-board - Rick Husband , William C. McCool , Michael P. Anderson , David M. Brown , Kalpana Chawla , Laurel Clark and Ilan Ramon - were all killed . <p> http : **40;15887;TOOLONG ... In 1945 , the final year of WWII , the leader of Nazi Germany Adolf Hitler began his residence in what became known as the F ? hrerbunker - an underground Berlin air raid shelter that formed the centre of the Nazi regime until Germany 's loss in the war and Hitler 's death.Hitler 's senior staff , family members , support , medical , and administrative staff were all also sheltered in the bunker . <p> http : **40;15929;TOOLONG ... As Nazi Germany 's position in the war rapidly deteriorated - despite Hitler 's attempts to refuse to believe so - he ultimately married Eva Braun in the bunker on 29 April 1945 less than 40 hours before they took their own lives in the same building.After the war , both the old and new Chancellery buildings of the bunker were levelled by the Soviets.Reconstruction of the area in Berlin that the bunker sat meant that it no longer exists at all today , with the site now housing a car park and some of the corridors that remain in shape underground off-limits to the public . <p> http : **40;15971;TOOLONG ... In 1947 , January 15 was the day the hideously mutilated remains of murdered American woman Elizabeth Short were found in a vacant lot in Los Angeles , sparking a police investigation that has since spanned seven decades.Since given the name ' Black Dahlia ' , Short 's heavily slashed , naked corpse was found severed in two at the waist , drained of blood , her face slashed from the mouth to her ears and her body " posed " , with her hands over her head , her elbows bent at right angles , and her legs spread apart . <p> http : **40;16013;TOOLONG ... Born in Boston , on the USA 's east coast , Short had moved to California as an aspiring actress , though she had no known acting credits or jobs during her time in Los Angeles.The Black Dahlia 's looks attracted the attention of media organisations and the public across the country and sparked widespread scrutiny of the case and of her character . <p> http : **40;16055;TOOLONG ... Short 's murder became the subject of a heavily intensive police investigation that focused on suspicious letters , anonymous phone calls , interviews and alleged suicide notes linked to the discovery of her remains , however her unsolved killing has still not yielded answers and the details surrounding it have generated various theories and public speculation for more than 70 years.The Black Dahlia murder has ever since been described as one of the USA 's most famous unsolved slayings , as well as one of the oldest unsolved cases in Los Angeles area . <p> http : **40;16097;TOOLONG ... January 15 also marked the day in 2009 that US Airways Flight 1549 remarkably landed in New York City 's Hudson River after hitting a flock of birds after takeoff and resorting to an emergency downing.After losing all power to the aircraft in the incident , pilots Chesley Sullenberger and Jeffrey Skiles were unable to reach any nearby airport and decided to glide the plane into the river . <p> http : **40;16139;TOOLONG ... The accident came to be known as the " Miracle on the Hudson " and a National Transportation Safety Board official described it as " the most successful ditching in aviation history " . The two pilots and flight attendants on-board were also awarded the Master 's Medal of the Guild of Air Pilots and Air Navigators in recognition of their " heroic and unique aviation achievement " . <p> http : **40;16181;TOOLONG ... Rescue ferries were able to reach the aircraft in the river within minutes and saved all 155 people on-board.Each passenger later received a letter of apology for the traumatic experience , US$5,000 in compensation for lost baggage and refund of their ticket price.The wreckage of the plane now remains in the Carolinas Aviation Museum in North Carolina . <p> http : **40;16223;TOOLONG ... In 1967 , American Football history was made on January 15 when the first-ever Super Bowl was played between the Kansas City Chiefs and the Green Bay Packers.Amid extensive rivalry between the USA 's two football leagues - the National Football League ( NFL ) and the American Football League ( AFL ) - the historic game was played at the Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum in front of a crowd of more than 61,000 . Coach Vince Lombardi 's Packers went on to win the title 35-10 after notching up 21 unanswered points in the second half alone , with quarterback Bart Starr named MVP . <p> http : **40;16265;TOOLONG ... In 1967 , British paleoanthropologist and archaeologist Louis Leakey announced what he claimed to be the discovery of the oldest known ancestor of man - dating back some 20 million years.Leakey 's work throughout his career was seen as revolutionary in demonstrating that humans evolved in Africa . <p> http : **40;16307;TOOLONG ... The first significant fossil linked to the human form and attributed to Leakey was a robust skull with huge teeth dated to 1.75 million years ago.That discovery was unveiled by Louis ' collaborator and second wife Mary Leakey , and it was ultimately named ' Zinjanthropus boisei ' . <p> http : **40;16349;TOOLONG ... While some saw Leakey 's work in Africa and his discoveries as controversial , it is generally accepted that he radically changed the way early humans are seen through the scientific lens . His work pushed back the known dates for the existence of various human species , stimulated research in new fields like primatology - which also inspired the careers of famed researchers such as Jane Goodall - and generated interest and publicity for the study of human origins . <p> http : **40;16391;TOOLONG ... January 14 was also the day in 1954 that world-famous actress , model and singer Marilyn Monroe married for the second time in her life , to American baseballer Joe DiMaggio.The couple 's marriage came at a time where Monroe was the focus of intense media publicity and scrutiny . <p> http : **40;16433;TOOLONG ... Despite their happy beginnings as a loved-up celebrity pairing , the Monroe-DiMaggio marriage was to be short-lived.After just nine months of wedlock , the couple were filed for divorce after DiMaggio 's jealousy and controlling attitude became an issue.Their separation was also flared by Monroe 's star role in Billy Wilder 's comedy ' The Seven Year Itch ' , which included the now-world-famous scene featuring the model standing over a subway grate with air blowing up the skirt of her white dress.That photoshoot , designed to spark public interest in the film , lasted for several hours and attracted nearly 2000 spectators , but also marked the end of her marriage to DiMaggio , who was infuriated by it . <p> http : **40;16475;TOOLONG ... In 1967 , January 14 was the day of the San Francisco Human Be-In that took part in the city 's Golden Gate Park Polo Fields as a prelude to the famous ' Summer of Love ' . The Be-In focused the key ideas of the 1960s counterculture and birthed the Hippie movement in the United States that saw up to 30,000 people flock to the park for the event.The movement stunned US media organisations who , at the time , did not believe such a feat was possible and also created the term " psychedelic " to a generation of youths . <p> http : **40;16517;TOOLONG ... A Manhattan court imposed sentences of up to 100 years for eight men named top leaders of the New York City Mafia . The three bosses of the " Five Families " who were among those jailed were Boss of the Genovese crime Family , Anthony " Fat Tony " Salerno ( pictured ) , Lucchese Family Boss Antonio " Tony Ducks " Corallo and leader of the Colombo Family , Carmine " Junior " Persico . <p> http : **40;16559;TOOLONG ... The case relied heavily upon a series of FBI bugs that recorded crime bosses discussing their secretive trade . The day the men heard their fate , the Bosses ate Italian food and had a bottle of wine in the court house holding area . January 10 also marks the day in 1962 that an avalanche occurred on Peru 's famous Huascar ? n mountain that rocked the Yungay region and killed thousands of people.It is believed the natural disaster was first sparked by a rapid rise in temperature in the area surrounding the mountain that unsettled snow on its surface . <p> http : **40;17903;TOOLONG ... In 2016 , the world was shocked by the sudden and untimely death of one of the most influential musicians of the 20th century - David Bowie , who lost his battle with liver cancer at the age of 69 . Acclaimed by critics and musicians around the world for his innovative creations , Bowie became one of the best-selling musicians on the globe during his career and was inducted into the Rock and Roll Hall of Fame in 1996 . <p> http : **40;17987;TOOLONG ... Bowie 's death came just two days after his 69th birthday - which was the day he also released his final-ever album ' Blackstar ' , and also after a lengthy battle with cancer.He had been diagnosed with the disease 18 months earlier but had not wanted the news of his illness made public.After the announcement of his death broke , murals and vigils around the world were created in his honour to showcase his music and multiple stages of his career - such as his ' Aladdin Sane ' persona seen here in his hometown of Brixotn , South London . <p> http : **40;18029;TOOLONG ... Following his death , Bowie 's producer Tony Visconti said that the musician had always : " did what he wanted to do . " " He wanted to do it his way and he wanted to do it the best way . His death was no different from his life -- a work of art , " he said at the time . " He made Blackstar for us , his parting gift . " He will always be with us . For now , it is appropriate to cry . " <p> http : **40;18071;TOOLONG ... Following his passing , sales of Bowie 's albums and singles worldwide skyrocketed.The musician , however , had always insisted that he did not want a funeral and so - after his cremation - his ashes were scattered in a Buddhist ceremony in Bali , Indonesia . <p> http : **40;18113;TOOLONG ... In 1920 , January 10 was the day that the Treaty of Versailles officially came into effect following the conclusion of World War I - finally confirming peace and the end of combat between Germany and the Allied Forces.The signing came following multiple meetings including representatives of the " Big Four " nations , or ( left to right ) : the UK 's David Lloyd George , Italy 's Vittorio Orlando , France 's Georges Clemenceau and the USA 's Woodrow Wilson.The Treaty has historically been seen as controversial , however , because - among its many provisions - one of the most significant inclusions was a requirement for Germany " to accept the responsibility of Germany and her allies for causing all the loss and damage " during the war.That single line in the Treaty meant that Germany had to disarm , give up large amounts of territory and pay reparations to certain countries estimated at a cost of 132 billion marks ( roughly AUD$618 billion in 2020 ) . <p> http : **40;18155;TOOLONG ... The Treaty was initially signed on June 28 the year before after six months of intense negotiations following the Armistice in the previous November , however it took another six months for it to come into effect around the world.The result of the period of tension and controversy - as well as competing and sometimes conflicting goals among the victors - was a compromise that left no one satisfied and Germany was neither pacified , conciliated or permanently weakened . <p> http : **40;18197;TOOLONG ... Eight hundred children were packed into the Laurier Palace Theatre in Montreal , Canada , on January 9 , 1927 , when a fire started.With the lights out and no exits marked , the children pushed against an inwards-opening door , with hundreds desperate to escape the smoke.Firefighters arrived from a station across the road , but were unable to get inside to save the children or extinguish the fire quickly . <p> http : **40;18239;TOOLONG ... In the end , 78 children died , including one from a fireman involved in the rescue , and three from a policeman.The tragedy became known as the " Saddest fire " . The cause of the fire was never discovered , but a discarded cigarette is often blamed . <p> http : **40;18281;TOOLONG ... The children were watching the Stan Laurel comedy Get ' Em Young.Grief in Montreal soon turned to anger , and Quebecois legislators soon banned children under 16 from attending cinemas.That law would not be overturned until 1961 . <p> http : **40;18323;TOOLONG ... One of the most notorious failures in military history , the Gallipoli campaign ended with a remarkable success.After more than nine months of bloodshed and stalemate , the decision was made to evacuate thousands of British , French and Anzac troops from the Turkish peninsula.But knowing that an evacuation could soon turn into a rout , it took extraordinary delicacy to slip away unnoticed . <p> http : **40;18365;TOOLONG ... This was largely thanks to the invention of William Scurry , a private from Carlton in Victoria.The " drip rifle " could be left in abandoned trenches but fire on its own , leading Turkish forces to believe there were still soldiers facing them . <p> http : **40;18407;TOOLONG ... More than 30,000 casualties were anticipated in the evacuation effort , but in the end , it was a tiny fraction of that.No Australians would be killed on the final night in Gallipoli.The final troops would leave the peninsula on January 9 , 1916 . <p> http : **40;18449;TOOLONG ... British authorities announced on January 9 , 1961 , that they had busted open a Soviet spy ring.MI5 got word from CIA that a mole had been passing on information from submarine tests in Portland , England.Suspicion fell on civil service clerk Harry Houghton , because of his profligate spending.Houghton and his mistress Ethel Gee were arrested and sentenced to 15 years in prison.After their release , they married . <p> http : **40;18491;TOOLONG ... In 1981 , January 8 marked the historic date of the " Trans-en-Provence Case " , in which a French farmer is said to have seen an Unidentified Flying Object that left behind physical evidence on his property.At the scene , as depicted here in an artist 's impression drawn after the incident , 55-year-old Renato Nicola ? claimed to have heard a strange whistling sound about 5pm . He then said he saw a saucer-shaped object about two metres in diameter land about 46 metres away . <p> http : **40;18575;TOOLONG ... Nicola ? later told French space investigators that " the device had the shape of two saucers , one inverted on top of the other . " This device had a ridge all the way around its circumference . Under the machine I saw two kinds of pieces as it was lifting off . They could be reactors or feet , " he said . " There were also two other circles which looked like trapdoors . The two reactors , or feet , extended about 20cm below the body of the machine . " The farmer also said that the object took off almost immediately , but burn marks on the ground where it had supposedly sat - marked out here with the letters A , B and C. <p> http : **40;18617;TOOLONG ... French space investigators from a body known as GEPAN , soon arrived on the property and spoke to the farmer before taking soil samples to test his theory.The analysis of those samples showed that the ground had been flattened by a mechanical pressure of about four to five tonnes and heated to between 300 and 600C . Phosphate and zinc were also found in the samples , and chlorophyll levels were between 30 and 50 per cent lower than expected . <p> http : **40;18659;TOOLONG ... Investigators were told Nicola ? first thought the device was a secret military tool , because of his close living proximity to an army base.GEPAN never confirmed this was the case and the exact reasoning behind what the saucer-like object may have been has never been completely discovered.Despite that , media organisations at the time labelled the incident as " perhaps the most completely and carefully documented sighting of all time " . <p> http : **40;18701;TOOLONG ... In 1931 , Australian aviator Guy Menzies completed the world 's first-ever solo trans-Tasman flight from Sydney to the west coast of New Zealand in an Avro Sports Avian aircraft that had been first transported from England.The daring mission almost never happened after Menzies began to fear that he might be denied permission for the trip by air authorities.Before embarking , he decided to tell the authorities and his family that he was flying to Perth . <p> http : **40;18827;TOOLONG ... At 1am on January 7 however , Menzies flew out of Sydney on his own.The voyage took 11 hours and 45 minutes and his brave effort remains solidified in history solely for his landing into New Zealand - heavily affected by poor weather he crash-landed upside-down in the La Fontaine Swamp near Hari Hari on the nation 's western coastline . <p> http : **40;18869;TOOLONG ... Despite the landing , the Australian aviator smashed the previous record set for a trans-Tasman flight by Charles Kingsford Smith and Charles Ulm in 1928 by two-and-a-half hours.Later in life , he went on to become a squadron leader in World War II and was killed when his flying boat was shot down over the Mediterranean Sea . <p> http : **40;18911;TOOLONG ... In 2015 , the Charlie Hebdo terror shooting attacked shocked both France and the world when masked gunman fired their weapons on the satirical newspaper 's headquarters in Paris.As the attack unfolded , horrific footage was shared online from shell shocked residents witnessing it happen.In the video , the gunmen armed with assault rifles are seen running up to an injured police officer , who lies squirming on the ground . The police officer raises his hands up before one of the assailants shoots him in the head . <p> http : **40;18953;TOOLONG ... The attackers - two brothers Sa ? d and Ch ? rif Kouachi - forced their way into the offices , killed 12 people and injured 11 more.The pair identified themselves as part of the Islamic terrorist group which took responsibility for the shootings.The attack was so brazen that it sent shockwaves around the world , characterised by the French slogan " Je Suis Charlie " ( I am Charlie ) , which mourners adorned to show that the shootings could have happened to anyone , and therefore they were in grief collectively as a whole . <p> http : **40;18995;TOOLONG ... That global outpouring of support ultimately led to the gathering of about two million people , including more than 40 world leaders , in Paris on January 11 for a rally of national unity , and 3.7 million people joined demonstrations across France.The remaining staff of Charlie Hebdo continued weekly publication , and their next issue following the shootings had 7.95 million copies in six languages produced . The mini-Holden , released throughout Australia in 1976 and modelled on General Motors ' new English Vauxhall Viva , but with stronger body and suspension , and round headlights instead of the square ones on the Viva . <p> http : **40;55612;TOOLONG ... The Camira is the product of five years of involvement in GM 's world J-car program , and four years of intense development work in Australia . The Camira completes the GMH model lineup by slotting neatly between the Gemini and Commodore in both size and price . This picture is from December 03 , 1982 . <p> http : **40;55654;TOOLONG ... The 48-215 was the first fully Australian assembled car built for the Australian domestic market . <p> http : **40;55696;TOOLONG ... @@71701717 <p> Nobody should be surprised that US President Donald Trump 's Middle East peace proposal is heavily tilted towards the Israelis . What is surprising is that , rather than rejecting Trump 's proposal , the world has left the Palestinians largely on their own . <p> While dismissing the Palestinians ' national yearnings with the patronising promise to ' improve their lives ' , the Trump peace plan embraces Israel 's national narrative that it alone has valid historical claims to Judea and Samaria , the Jews ' biblical homeland . So , while it would give the Palestinians their own state , it would be a fragmented territory covering Gaza and 70% of a West Bank dotted with Israeli settlements and surrounded on all sides by Israeli-annexed territory . Its capital would be located in a suburb of East Jerusalem , which would remain Israel 's undivided capital . <p> The plan ignores Palestinian demands for the right of return to homes left when Israel was established in 1948 -- a right that was recognised at the time by United Nations General Assembly resolution 194 . Moreover , in line with Israel 's far-right ethnocratic ambitions , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 300,000 Israeli Arabs of their citizenship , making them citizens of the Palestinian state . <p> The Palestinian territories would be connected by a maze of tunnels and bridges , and much like the Bantustans of apartheid South Africa , they would be subject to overwhelming Israeli military control . Anyone entering the new Palestine -- by air , sea or land -- would have to go through Israel . <p> Meanwhile , Palestinians would be prohibited from creating their own armed forces or engaging in any activities ' that adversely affect the State of Israel 's security ' -- as determined by Israel 's government . Likewise , the Palestinians would have to earn the right to this sham state by disarming Hamas and proving -- not to any international body , but to Israel and the United States -- that they have created a democratic system governed by the rule of law . <p> In presenting such a deeply unfair plan , Trump has destroyed any credibility that the US may have still had as an honest mediator . Moreover , he has undermined the belief -- crucial @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ principles of the Israeli -- Palestinian peace process , from the need to adhere to the pre-1967 borders to the illegality of Israeli settlements , are unassailable . <p> Given how willing the rest of the world seems to be to abandon the Palestinians , these losses will not be easy to reverse , even if Trump loses the November presidential election and his successor abandons the plan , as candidates like Bernie Sanders and Elizabeth Warren seem prepared to do . The fact that anyone is taking Trump 's skewed plan seriously is a testament , first and foremost , to the radical transformation that the Middle East has undergone in recent years . Solidarity with the Palestinians used to be the glue that held together an otherwise fragmented Arab world ; now , their plight is seen as a nuisance , a burden and above all an obstacle to rapprochement with Israel . <p> Yes , the Arab League repudiated the US plan at a meeting of its foreign ministers in Cairo . But the truth , as Lebanon 's Hezbollah movement put it , is that Trump @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ without the ' complicity and betrayal ' of several Arab states . <p> The ambassadors of Bahrain , Oman and the United Arab Emirates participated in the White House unveiling ceremony , implying these countries ' approval . Moreover , despite ' renewing its assertion ' of support for the Palestinian people , Saudi Arabia noted that it ' values the efforts ' of the Trump administration in ' developing a comprehensive peace deal between the Israeli and Palestinian sides ' . Given Trump 's apparent loyalty to Saudi Arabia -- based , not least , on lucrative arms deals -- the kingdom 's crown prince , Mohammed bin Salman , was probably privy to the plan 's contents before it was released . <p> In Jordan , King Abdullah initially warned that Israel 's annexation of the Jordan Valley would have serious security consequences . But he has since advised other countries to ' look at the glass half full ' . Judging by its ambiguous attitude towards Palestinian statehood , one must wonder whether Jordan would prefer having as its neighbour Israel , with which it has historically @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Palestinian state . <p> Similarly , Egypt 's foreign ministry called upon both sides ' to carefully consider ' the peace plan , and even claimed that creating an independent and sovereign state in the occupied territories would restore all the ' legitimate rights ' of Palestinians . And though Tunisia 's president condemned the peace plan as ' the injustice of the century ' , the country 's ambassador to the UN was soon sacked for leading the challenge against it in the Security Council . <p> But it is not only the Arab world that is letting down the Palestinians . The European Union 's high representative for foreign affairs and security policy , Josep Borrell , strongly condemned the plan . However , his unilateral statement was needed precisely because some EU countries -- including the Czech Republic , Hungary and Italy -- blocked a shared resolution . France , for its part , gave the plan a cautious welcome . <p> Beyond the EU , the UK government hailed the Trump peace plan as ' a serious proposal ' and ' a positive step forward ' @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ UN resolutions ' , Russia stands to gain from what a Moscow Times editorial called the ' unilaterally imposed humiliating terms ' of the deal . This ' provides a precedent for major powers dictating terms to weaker ones ' and vindicates Russia 's occupation of Crimea , ' Russia 's West Bank ' . In fact , ' limited sovereignty ' was exactly what the Kremlin wanted to give the former Soviet republics . <p> If , with the probable connivance of the Trump administration , Israel annexes its West Bank settlements , the paradigm shift in the Israeli -- Palestinian peace process would be complete . Israel 's status as an apartheid state would be officially confirmed . And the Palestinians will face a stark choice : submit or struggle . If they choose the latter , it had better be a diplomatic struggle ; a third intifada would be calamitous for their cause . Boycotting America , still an indispensable power for an equitable regional solution , proved to be a wrongheaded Palestinian policy .
